[1755] TOOLRESULT: fragment: bidirectional-relation
diagram: { kinds: [entity], members: types }
last_updated: 2026-11-10
---
## Intent

Two entities need to navigate to each other and the platform should keep both ends consistent. Declare the relation as a two-way relation: two members that name each other as `partner`.

## Practice

### Declare both ends and point them at each other

- A TwoWayRelationMember exists only on entities, never on transfer objects. Each end names the other as its `partner`. The two ends may have different cardinalities: a Customer has `0..*` orders, an Order has `0..1` customer.
- Setting the relation from either side changes the other, because it is one stored relation seen from two ends. There is no second column to keep in step.
- Many-to-many is allowed (`0..*` on both ends). Exactly one end of each pair is `primary`, which resolves ownership of the stored relation; project practice is to make the "many"/owning side primary.

### Relation kinds and the validator

The platform's validator enforces these rules (see [Relation kinds per layer and the bidirectional rules](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md#pattern-relation-kinds-per-layer-and-the-bidirectional-rules) in the agent-docs):

- A two-way relation cannot be a COMPOSITION on either end.
- The partner of an AGGREGATION end must be an ASSOCIATION. If you use AGGREGATION, put it on the collection (owner) end and ASSOCIATION on the back-pointer. ASSOCIATION on both ends is also valid.
- At le

[1812] TOOLRESULT: export async function fillEnumCombobox(page: Page, locator: Page | Locator, label: ElementNameType, option: string, backCheck=true ) {
    if (!(await locator.getByRole('option', { name: option }).isVisible())) {
        await locator.getByLabel(label).first().click();
        await page.waitForTimeout(200);
    }
    await locator.getByRole('option', { name: option }).click();
    await page.waitForTimeout(200);
    backCheck && await expect(locator.getByLabel(label)).toHaveValue(option);
}

export async function checkEnumCombobox( s: Page | Locator, label: ElementNameType, option: string ) {
    await expect(s.getByLabel(label)).toHaveValue(option);
}

export async function fillSingleRelationElement(page: Page, locator: Page | Locator, name: ElementNameType, value: string ) {
        await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, locator.getByRole('combobox', { name: name }), /.*range$/);
        await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithAction(page, locator.getByRole('combobox', { name: name }).first().fill(value), /.*range$/);
        await page.getByRole('option', { name: value }).first().click();
        await page.waitForTimeout(200);
}

[1842] TOOLRESULT: ### When No Manual Step Is Needed

Adding a **stored** member (for example a new attribute) to an entity needs **no** manual migration or `schema-upgrade` run for a fresh or development database: the generated Liquibase changelog (`application/model/target/generated-resources/model/<app>-liquibase_postgresql.changelog.xml`) already contains the new column. A manual schema-upgrade step is only relevant for a persistent database that already holds data, and for genuinely non-additive or backward-incompatible changes (renames, type changes, removals). For a local persistent PostgreSQL database `./judo.sh schema-upgrade` generates the difference against the running database and applies it.

**⚠️ IMPORTANT: When Schema Migration is Required**

Schema migration is **ONLY necessary when entity types change** in the model. The JUDO platform generates SQL schema from entity type definitions.

**Schema migration IS required when:**
- ✅ Adding/removing/modifying entity types (mapped entities)
- ✅ Adding/removing attributes on entity types
- ✅ Changing attribute types (String → Integer, etc.)
- ✅ Adding/removing relationships between entities
- ✅ Modifying entity inheritance hierarchy

**Schema migration is NOT required when:**
- ❌ Changing transfer object types (view models, DTOs)
- ❌ Modifying actors or access points
- ❌ Changing UI layouts or forms
- ❌ Adding/removing operations (unless they affect entity structure)
- ❌ Modifying validations or derived attributes (computed fields)

**

[1848] TOOLRESULT: upgrade_postgresql_schema () {
    mvnd judo-rdbms-schema:apply \
    ${mavenVersionArg} \
    -DjdbcUrl=jdbc:postgresql://127.0.0.1:${POSTGRES_PORT:-5432}/${schema_name} \
    -DdbType=postgresql \
    -DdbUser=${schema_name} \
    -DdbPassword=${schema_name} \
    -DschemaIgnoreModelDependency=true \
    -DupdateModel=${SCHEMA_DIR}/model/target/generated-resources/model/${schema_name}-rdbms_postgresql.model \
    -f ${SCHEMA_DIR}/schema || exit
}

install_maven_wrapper () {
    mvn wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.8.6 || exit
    sed $(get_sed_edit_option) 's/https:\/\/nexus\.judo\.technology\/repository\/maven-judong\//https:\/\/repo\.maven\.apache\.org\/maven2\//; s/https\:\/\//####/; s/\/\//\//; s/####/https\:\/\//' ${MODEL_DIR}/.mvn/wrapper/maven-wrapper.properties
}

prune_application () {
    if [ $pruneConfirmation -eq 1 ]; then
        [ $pruneFrontend -eq 1 ] && location="frontend-react" || location="this repository"
        echo -ne "Prune command deletes all untracked files in $location!\nAre you sure you want to continue? [Y/n]: "
        read canContinue
    else
        canContinue="Y"
    fi

    if [ "$canContinue" == "Y" ]; then
        if [ $pruneFrontend -eq 1 ]; then
            git clean -dffx ${APP_DIR}/frontend-react
        else
schema_upgrade () {
    if [ $postgres -eq 1 ]; then
        start_postgres postgres-${schema_name} $POSTGRES_PORT
        wait_for_port 127.0.0.1 ${POSTGRES_PORT:-5432} 30
        upgrade_postgresql_schema
    fi
}

### TabularReferenceField (ui:TabularReferenceField)

**Supertypes:** `Tabular` + `ReferenceField` → (`VisualElement`, `Widget` + `PerformableAction`)

A complex widget for managing relationships (to-one or to-many).

| ESM Element | ESM Attribute | UI Model | Frontend Effect |
|-------------|---------------|----------|-----------------|
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `name` | Table name | Component name |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `label` | Header | Table title (i18n) |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `iconName` | Icon | Header icon |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `col` | Grid column (1-12) | Layout width |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `relationFeature` (ref) | Bound relation | Data binding to RelationMember |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `representationComponent` | Display type | `table`/`tag`/`card`/`button` |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `buttonStyle` | Button style | `text`/`contained`/`outlined` (when `representationComponent=button`) |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `smallTable` | Compact mode | Dense table without pagination |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `checkboxSelection` | Selection mode | `AUTO`/`ENABLED`/`DISABLED` |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `countRows` | Show row count | Display total row count |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `isInlineEditable` | Inline editing | Enable inline row editing |
| `ui:TabularReferenceField` | `rowsPerPage` | Page size | Pagination limit (default: 10) |

[1922] TOOLRESULT: #### Pattern: Conditional UI Element Visibility
*   **Use Case**: A UI element (field, group, button) should only be visible or enabled based on other data in the form.
*   **Solution**: Use `enabledBy` or `hiddenBy` on any `ui:VisualElement`, pointing it to a `Boolean` `DataMember`.

#### Pattern: Alternative Data Representations
*   **Use Case**: Display a list as a grid of cards, chips, or an autocomplete field instead of a table.
*   **Solution**: Use the `representationComponent` attribute on a `TabularReferenceField` (`"table"`, `"tag"`, `"card"` or `"button"`). A dropdown/autocomplete selector for a relation is a separate widget (`isTypeAheadField`, `autocompleteRows`), not a `representationComponent` value.

### Advanced Data Modeling Patterns

#### Pattern: Polymorphism with Inheritance
The bound `relationFeature` shape determines which attributes are valid.

| Relation shape | Enabled attributes | `representationComponent` options |
|---|---|---|
| `upper=-1` (to-many) | `rowsPerPage`, `isInlineEditable`, `smallTable`, `checkboxSelection`, `countRows` | `table`, `card`, `tag` |
| `upper=1` (to-one) | `autocompleteRows`, `buttonStyle` | `table`, `button`, `tag` |
| `relationKind=COMPOSITION` | CRUD buttons = {create, delete}; `smallTable` valid | — |
| `relationKind=AGGREGATION` | CRUD buttons = {add, remove} (selector dialog); `smallTable` valid | — |
| `relationKind=ASSOCIATION` | typically add/remove via selector | — |

`crudOperationsDisplayed` and `transferOpera

[1957] ASSISTANT: ## Round 6 report: Level 1 (F01–F08)

All 8 functions are built. The full suite gave 29 passed and 1 skipped (the empty-state test from Round 5). It was run once at the end, after fixing failures along the way. Everything is committed locally as `836c3d6`.

### What I built, per function
All of it is in the model, plus the frontend extension and the seeding.
- **F01:** a `Category` entity, a `CategoryInfo` transfer object, and an access `categories` with create, update and delete. There is a "Categories" menu entry and a generated table, form and view.
- **F02:** `sku` (a `SKU` type), a `Unit` enumeration, and a `category` relation on `Product`, shown in the product form, view and table. Cards on the home grid now show the category chip and SKU. The details page shows SKU, Unit and Category as generated fields below the custom layout.
- **F03:** `sku` and `unit` are required, in addition to name. Saving without a SKU shows "Missing required attribute."
- **F04:** `sku` is an identifier attribute. A second product with `FW-1001` is rejected in the create dialog, which stays open and shows an error.
- **F05:** the `SKU` type has the pattern `^[A-Z]{2}-[0-9]{4}$` and a maximum length of 20. `ab1234` is rejected.
- **F06:** `Unit` is an enumeration with PIECE, KG and LITRE. The form shows a dropdown with exactly 3 options.
- **F07:** the product table has columns for Name, Price, SKU, Unit and "Price (number)". It has filters for Name, SKU, Unit and "Price (number)". I set labels on the filters (they defaulted to "NameFilter"). The tests cover the name filter, numeric sort and paging.
- **F08:** `Product.category` and `Category.products` are a two-way relation. The category view has a "Products" button that opens that category's products, and a product shows a Category link back.
- **Seed data through the UI:** `a-seed-catalog.spec.ts` creates 3 categories (Footwear, Socks, Bags) and 8 products with SKUs, units and 2–3 line descriptions. It is idempotent and removes leftover "Temp …" categories first.
- **Level 0 tests updated:** the earlier tests now expect 8 products.
- **TRACE.md:** updated per function, and it says what I read and what I only searched.

### Catalog paths used
- `agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/structure.md`, `type.md`, `accesspoint.md`
- `agent-docs/model/judo-cli.md`, `model-development.md`, `ui-authoring-guide.md`
- `agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md`, `tables-navigation.md`
- `best-practices/model/bidirectional-relation.md`
- `agent-docs/deployment/schema-evolution.md`
- `agent-docs/e2e-testing/writing-tests.md`
- Not read: `backend/type-system-guide.md`. `backend/platform-behavior-reference.md` was not found by `kb_get`.

### Issues
1. **The existing database was not migrated. Platform script bug plus catalog gap (R6-1).**
   - **What happened:** after adding `sku` and the relation, every product query failed with `column c_sku does not exist`. `schema-evolution.md` says a development database needs no manual step, and `schema-upgrade` is listed for persistent databases.
   - **Why `schema-upgrade` failed:** it uses `-f ${SCHEMA_DIR}/schema`, and `SCHEMA_DIR` defaults to the project root, so the module path is wrong. With `-o schema_dir=application` it hung for 15 minutes.
   - **What I did:** I removed the postgres and keycloak containers and both named volumes, restarted, recreated the Keycloak user with `scripts/seed-dev-user.sh`, and re-seeded through the UI.
   - **What was lost:** all Level 0 test data. It is recreated by the seed test.
2. **A to-one relation shows as a plain button when it is an association. Catalog gap (R6-2).**
   - **Symptom:** the form could not set a category.
   - **Fix:** with `relationKind=AGGREGATION` on `ProductInfo.category`, the form shows an autocomplete combobox.
   - **Kb gap:** the docs list `table`, `button` and `tag` options for `representationComponent`, but the CLI only accepts `table`.
   - **Constraint:** both relations as aggregation fail valida

[1978] TOOLRESULT: # Webshop: required functions, by increasing JUDO complexity

This is the acceptance list for the catalog test, written before anything is built. The app is built only from the
JUDO catalog (kb search over `judo-blueprint/catalog`) and the generated project's own docs.

Each level adds one JUDO capability on top of the previous levels. That way, a failure points at the
platform feature, or the catalog guidance, that broke.

- **JUDO feature:** the platform mechanism the function relies on.
- **Catalog:** where the guidance should come from. A missing or wrong entry is recorded in `GAPS.md`.
- **Browser check:** what a tester does in the running Docker stack, and what must happen.

## Frontend quality bar (applies to every level)

The shop must look and behave like a finished product, not like a raw generated back office. Every level's browser checks include these rules:

- **Built on the JUDO frontend.** Use the generated JUDO React frontend. Customise it only through its extension points: hooks, the `src/custom` folder, theming, i18n and extra routes (agent-docs/frontend/extension-points.md, frontend/hooks/, frontend/theming.md, frontend/i18n.md).
- **No patched generated files.** Never edit a generated file or add it to `.generator-ignore`. If no extension point can do the job, record that in `GAPS.md` and ask before patching.
- **Custom components where the generated UI is not enough**, e.g. a product card grid, a price display, a details layout. Register them through hook

## Intent

Give each actor type its own view of the same domain entities. An administrator sees the full entity. A restricted actor sees a smaller transfer object with fewer fields, read-only derived fields and only the access it needs.

### Projections are per actor, not per entity

Each actor gets its own transfer objects over the shared entities. The entities s

[1995] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md", "section": "Advanced JUDO Modeling Patterns > 2. Security & UI Patterns > Pattern: Mapped Managed Principal with Claim Mapping"}
[1995] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/backend/authentication-guide.md", "section": "Authentication Guide > Principal-to-Actor Resolution and Multi-Client Mapping > Several Keycloak clients in one realm"}
[1996] TOOLRESULT: *   **Use Case**: You want the logged-in user to correspond to a **persisted row** in your domain model (e.g. a `User` entity carrying roles, permission flags, preferences, audit fields), and you want JUDO to keep that row in sync with the IdP automatically — no manual "just-in-time user provisioning" interceptor for the common case.
*   **Problem**: Without claim mapping the only way to reach identity data is the raw JWT via `USER.*`. That forces every access filter and custom op to re-derive the user row from a JWT claim (`User!filter(u | u.email == String!getVariable('USER', 'email'))!any()`), and gives you no persisted identity at all — making roles, audit, and per-user flags impossible to model.
*   **Pattern**: Combine three ESM features on the `accesspoint` side so the framework takes over provisioning and exposes the persisted row to JQL as `ACTOR.*`:
    1.  **`ActorType.principal`** points at a **mapped** `TransferObjectType` that projects the `EntityType` that stores the user row (e.g. a `User` TO mapped to `User` entity).
    2.  **`ActorType.managed = true`**. On `#_principal` the framework is *intended* to insert (first login) or update (subsequent logins) the principal row from the JWT. **In practice the auto-insert is NOT implemented in the current runtime** — see the warning below — so you must pair `managed=true` with an `AuthenticationInterceptor` that performs the first-login insert. The lookup path that consumes the row IS implemented, so once the interce
[1997] TOOLRESULT: 
JUDO derives the actor from the token's `azp` claim: dashes become dots and the result must equal an actor FQN. A frontend client with another name cannot be resolved. Set the whitelist `JUDO_PLATFORM_ACCEPTABLE_CLIENTS` (OSGi/Guice key `acceptableClients`, default empty):

```
ActorTypeFQN=client1,client2;OtherActor=client3
```

Client ids are normalised (dash to dot) and an ambiguous client-to-actor mapping is rejected. The Keycloak login interceptor also accepts a `clientToActorMap` that renames `azp` values to actor names. See [OSGi Runtime Services](../deployment/osgi-runtime-services.md#keycloak-connector-and-realm-synchronizer-configuration) for the realm side.

[2001] TOOLRESULT: ## AuthenticationInterceptor Interface

The core of this pattern is the `AuthenticationInterceptor` interface. Your custom interceptor will implement this interface to hook into the authentication lifecycle.

```java
public interface AuthenticationInterceptor {
    String getName();
    boolean isSuitableForOperation(EOperation operation, String claim, String realm, String client, Map<String, Object> attributes);
    void authenticate(String operationFQName, Map<String, Object> exchange, String claim, String realm, String client, Map<String, Object> attributes);
}
```

## The `#_principal` Operation

The `#_principal` operation is a special, framework-level operation that is automatically invoked after a user successfully authenticates with an external identity provider. This is the primary point of interception for just-in-time provisioning.

**Key Characteristics:**
- **Triggered Automatically**: Called by the JUDO framework after a successful external login.
- **Provides Token Claims**: The `attributes` map in the `authenticate` method contains all the claims from the user's JWT token.
- **Ideal for User Creation**: It's the perfect place to check if a user exists in your local database and create them if they don't.

## Example: Just-in-Time User Provisioning

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **Model-driven provisioning comes first — check the actor shape before writing an interceptor.**
>
> If the `ActorType` in your ESM is **mapped + `managed="true"` + has `<claims>`** (see [Mapped Man

[2010] TOOLRESULT: 
Operations that accept an uploaded file or return a generated one (CSV, JSON, DOCX, templates, credentials) go through the FileStore instead of passing bytes through the operation signature. The client holds a file reference; the backend reads and writes content by id.

## Practice

### Platform base: binary members and the token flow

In the model a file is a binary-typed member (binary types carry a MIME type and a maximum size; see [JSL language notes](../../agent-docs/model/jsl-language-notes.md)). An operation that returns a file wraps the binary in a transient member of an unmapped transfer object ([wrapper transfer object for binary outputs](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md)). Over REST the client uses a two-phase token flow: it requests `<attributePa

The backend storage is selected by environment variables (`JUDO_PLATFORM_FILESTORE` with `filesystem`, plus `JUDO_PLATFORM_FILESTORE_DIRECTORY` for the directory; see [application config](../../agent-docs/deployment/application-config.md)). The agent-docs also describe an S3 backend whose access URL is an internal `<protocol>:<fileId>-<filename>` URL, so downloads flow through the Karaf servlet ([filestore S3 details](../../agent-docs/deployment/

### Project practice: read by id, write and return a reference

[2012] TOOLRESULT: - **Document** is not createable, updateable or deleteable through the generated interface. It holds a binary `file` (required), an `uploadedAt` timestamp and an optional `description`. It refers to its parent (0..1, association).
- **DocumentInput** (service layer, not drawn in the diagram) is an unmapped transfer object with the `file` field. It is the input of a custom upload operation.
- **DocumentTransfer** (service layer, not drawn in the diagram) is a mapped transfer object for viewing: it exposes the file and the metadata. A binary field mapped on a create-capable transfer gets a generated upload-token operation, so a plain create through the parent needs no custom upload operation.

The parent entity composes or associates the document. The document itself is created through a parent operation, not by the client directly.

Variants that add a `type` or a version attribute, or a URL attribute in place of the binary file, are described in the entry's BLUEPRINT. The URL variant stores a reference to an externally hosted file.

Binary values travel as file-store tokens on the wire, not as bytes in the JSON.

Generated UI pages follow from the entities. The document is non-CRUD, so keep the view and table pages on the parent and hide the generated create and edit forms for Document. Customize the upload action on the parent so it sends a DocumentInput.

[2016] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Show a stored binary (image, document) inside custom views where the generated widgets are not used. The file download needs the token header, so a plain `src` link to the file cannot work; the hook fetches the bytes and hands the view a local object URL.

## Practice

### Platform base

- Binary attributes hold a token, and the generated access service downloads the file with the token and a disposition (`inline` or `attachment`) ([custom attachments: token flow](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md)). The download path is a separate authenticated request, so it cannot be used directly as an element `src` (*verify* for your generated version).

### Project practice: one hook owns the URL lifecycle (mlszksz-platform)

- The hook takes a token (or nothing) and returns a string URL or `null`. It creates the access service once with `useMemo`, using the project's shared axios provider.
- The effect first revokes the URL held in a ref, then, if there is a token, downloads in `inline` mode, wraps the response data in a `Blob` with the response content type and stores the new object URL in the ref and in state.
- A local `cancelled` flag is set in the effect cleanup; a response arriving after a token change or unmount does not set state.
- Cleanup revokes the URL held in the ref. The ref, not the state value, is used so the cleanup always sees the latest URL.
- Errors are logged and the hook returns `null`; the caller shows a placeholder.

[2018] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Restrict what an actor can reach by giving its access a `DERIVED` type and a getter expression, so visibility is enforced in the model.

## Practice

- An actor access has `accessType` `ALL` or `DERIVED`. `ALL` is fixed to `0..*` and has no getter; `DERIVED` has a configurable cardinality (`0..1` or `0..*`) and a getter ([accesspoint.md](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md), *AccessType Behaviour*).
- Getter rules, all at once ([architectural patterns](../../agent-docs/backend/architectural-patterns.md), *Rules for `accesses[*].getterExpression`*):
  - the filter starts from the entity, not from the transfer object
  - `self` is not in scope inside an actor access getter; iterate from the entity with a lambda variable
  - a `DERIVED` access needs an expression that returns references; for "see everything" use `ALL` with no getter
  - `!getVariable(...)` returns a scalar; compare with `==`, and qualify the primitive type
- Typical shapes:
  - status filter: `Contest!filter(c | c.status == ContestStatus#OPEN)`
  - singleton: an expression ending in `!any()` with cardinality `0..1`
  - ownership: compare an entity field to the actor's own value, for example `!getVariable("ACTOR", "email")`
  - role-scoped: one `!filter` whose lambda disjuncts a role flag and the ownership clause; JQL has no if/else ([advanced modeling patterns](../../agent-docs/model/advanced-modeling-patterns.md), *Role-scoped access getters*)
- Inside a derived attribute on the princ

[2030] TOOLRESULT: ## Mandatory rules

These rules are **platform invariants**, not preferences. Violating them produces incorrect or destructive results.

1. **Never read the `.model` XML file directly.** Always go through CLI queries. The XML is too large to safely read in attention, and any analysis that bypasses the GraphQL view is unverified.

2. **Never edit the `.model` XML file directly.** Even tiny hand edits can break `xmi:id` references in distant parts of the file. Always go through CLI mutations.

3. **Always paginate query results.** Use `limit`, `offset`, and `totalCount` together. Without explicit pagination, large result sets are silently truncated and the assistant operates on a partial view of the model.

4. **One writer per model file.** A model file is changed by exactly one CLI session at a time. Never point two agents, two terminals or a CLI and the Designer at the same `.model` file: each holds its own in-memory copy and the last `save` silently overwrites the other's edits.

5. **Parallel model work happens in separate worktrees, joined with `merge`.** Give every parallel task its own git worktree (or its own copy of the model file), all started from the same commit. Each task edits and saves only its own copy. Then, in the target worktree, run `merge <theirs.model> --base <base.model>` once per copy, where `<base.model>` is the file all copies started from (for example `git show <start-commit>:model/<app>.model > /tmp/base.model`). Without `--base`, elements both copie

[2050] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Different roles need different experiences: back-office users want fast generated CRUD, customers or players want a tailored UI. The platform generates one frontend module per actor, so each can be customized to a different degree on top of one shared backend.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A frontend module is generated per modeled actor, named `<app>__<actor path>` (for example `<app>__service__admin`) under `application/frontend-react/`, next to a shared `model/` folder that holds the generated UI model. Each module has its own `.generator-ignore`, `src/custom/` folder and hook scaffolds ([frontend guide](../../agent-docs/frontend/README.md)).
- What an actor sees is defined by its accesses and menu in the model; each access becomes a navigation entry and page of that actor's frontend ([navigation controller and actor accesses](../../agent-docs/frontend/model-screen-layout.md)).
- The backend resolves the actor from the token's `azp` claim (dashes become dots, the result must equal an actor FQN). A frontend client with another name needs the `JUDO_PLATFORM_ACCEPTABLE_CLIENTS` whitelist; each actor exposes `~meta` and `~principal` endpoints that a custom frontend uses to obtain a token ([authentication guide: several Keycloak clients in one realm](../../agent-docs/backend/authentication-guide.md)).
- Customization options per module, from least to most: leave scaffolds inactive; activate hooks from `src/custom/`; sub-themes and custom visual elements; extra r

import Box from '@mui/system/Box';
import { type CSSProperties, useEffect, useMemo, useState } from 'react';
import { AccessServiceImpl } from '~/services/data-axios/AccessServiceImpl';
import { judoAxiosProvider } from '~/services/data-axios/JudoAxiosProvider';
import { fileHandling } from '~/utilities/file-handling';

export interface ImageDisplayProps {
  token: string;
  imageCSS?: CSSProperties;
  sx?: SxProps;
  fallbackUrl?: string;
}

export function ImageDisplay({ token, imageCSS, sx, fallbackUrl }: ImageDisplayProps) {
  const { extractFileNameFromToken } = fileHandling();

  const [imageUrl, setImageUrl] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [altText, setAltText] = useState<string | null>(null);

  const accessServiceImpl = useMemo(() => new AccessServiceImpl(judoAxiosProvider), []);

  useEffect(() => {
    (async () => {
      if (token) {
        try {
          const response = await accessServiceImpl.downloadFile(token, 'inline');

          if (response && response.status === 200) {
            const fileType = response.headers['content-type'];
            const url = window.URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([response.data], { type: fileType }));
            setImageUrl(url);
            setAltText(extractFileNameFromToken(token));
          } else {
            setImageUrl(null);
            setAltText(null);
          }
        } catch (error) {
          console.error(error);
          setImageUrl(null);

async function login(page: Page) {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill('shop');
  await page.locator('#password').fill('shop');
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();
  await expect(page.getByTestId('catalog-grid').or(page.getByTestId('catalog-empty'))).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 }); // the shop home is the card grid
}

async function openProductList(page: Page) {
  await login(page);
  await navigateToAccess(page, 'Products');
  await expect(tableHelper.getRows(page).first()).toBeVisible();
}

[2207] TOOLRESULT: import { test, expect, Page } from '@playwright/test';
import { openProductList, login, shots, tableHelper, apiHelper } from './helpers-shop';

const FORBIDDEN = /ProductInfo|ShopUserInfo/;

async function noInternalNames(page: Page) {
  await expect(page.locator('body')).not.toContainText(FORBIDDEN);
}

async function fieldOrder(page: Page) {
  const boxes = [];
  for (const label of ['Name', 'Price', 'Description']) {
    const box = await page.getByRole('textbox', { name: new RegExp(label) }).boundingBox();
    expect(box, label).not.toBeNull();
    boxes.push(box!.y);
  }
  expect(boxes[0]).toBeLessThan(boxes[1]);
  expect(boxes[1]).toBeLessThan(boxes[2]);
}

async function distinctColumns(page: Page): Promise<number> {
  const xs = await page.getByTestId('product-card').evaluateAll((els) => els.map((e) => Math.round(e.getBoundingClientRect().left)));
  return new Set(xs).size;
}

[2320] TOOLRESULT: 
Let a composed child reach its parent without a stored foreign key, either through a derived relation to the parent or inline with `!container()`.

## Practice

- `!container(type)` returns the container of an instance when it is part of a `COMPOSITION` ([model-development](../../agent-docs/model/model-development.md), *Reference Functions*).
- Inline form: a derived attribute on the child such as `self!container(Order).number`.
- Relation form: a derived relation on the child, typed as the parent with cardinality `0..1`, whose getter is `self!container(Order)`. Calling it `container` is only a convention; domain names such as `owner` also occur.
- Both forms are read-only; both need the child to be composed in the parent. For a non-composition link use an ordinary two-way relation instead ([[bidirectional-relation]]).
- A derived relation with upper bound 1 needs its getter to end in a single instance; `!container()` already returns one, so no extra narrowing is needed here (collections would need `!any()`, see [model-development](../../agent-docs/model/model-development.md), *Using `!any()` correctly*).

## Rationale

The parent is already known from the composition, so storing it again duplicates data. The derived form costs a small computation on read and cannot be written. For platform-side code the generated DAO has a matching `queryContainer` for the child (see [interceptors](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md)).

## Example

`Order` composes `OrderLine` (`lines

[2347] ASSISTANT: ## Round 7 report: Level 2 (F09–F16), plus the F08 and F07 follow-ups

Level 2 is built and committed locally as `30914f0`. The last full runs were green:
- **Admin:** 30 passed. That includes the two expected `test.fail()` field-order cases, which count as passed.
- **Customer:** 15 passed and 1 skipped (the empty-state test from Round 5).
- **Caveat on the runs:** the whole suite did not run green in one pass. The admin and customer suites ran as separate commands, and the customer suite passed only after the F16 rewrite and an earlier test-code fix.
- **Late edits:** I did not re-run the admin suite after the last test edits, which were to the customer F16 test only.

### Follow-ups
- **F08:** the Footwear category page now lists its products in a table on the page itself. The kb and the CLI have no `isEager`. The documented lever is `representationComponent`, and the CLI default on a view is `button`. I set it to `table`. The test checks that no "Products" button exists and that the rows are inline.
- **F07:** the product table now has a **Category** column and filter, from the derived `categoryName` in F12. The `test.fail()` is removed.

### Built
- **F09 image:**
  - **Model:** the product has a binary `image` (PNG or JPEG, up to 2 MB).
  - **Seeding:** the seed uploads a generated PNG for each of the 8 products through the file chooser in the create dialog.
  - **Customer front:** the card grid and the details page show the real image. The placeholder art remains only for products without one.
  - **Admin:** the view shows the stored file name, not a picture, because the generated binary field is an upload control.
- **F10 variants:** Variant is composed in Product, with label, SKU and stock. Variants are added in an inline table and stored with Update.
- **F11 stock status:** `stockStatus` is derived (stock ≤ 0 gives "Out of stock", below 5 gives "Low", otherwise "In stock"), and nothing is stored. It shows in the admin table and on both details pages.
- **F12 category name:** `ProductInfo.categoryName` is derived from `self.category.name` and shows in the table.
- **F13 sign-up and login:**
  - **Registration:** self sign-up works through the Keycloak registration page.
  - **First login:** `CustomerProvisioningInterceptor` creates the `CustomerAccount` row on the first customer login. It never blocks a login and never provisions Admin.
- **F14 two actors:**
  - **Admin:** back office with Products and Categories, at `/webshop/Admin`.
  - **Customer:** shop front with Products, My profile and My addresses, at `/webshop/Customer`. The Level 0 grid, details layout, Price component, labels and theme moved to Customer.
  - **Spillover:** a customer who opens the back office sees "User not found".
- **F15 active only:** the customer's `products` access filters `Product!filter(p | p.active)`. Admin sees everything.
- **F16 own data:**
  - **Profile:** the customer's profile is the singleton `CustomerAccount!filter(c | c.email == getVariable('ACTOR','email'))`.
  - **Addresses:** the profile embeds an addresses table. A flat "My addresses" list sits beside it.
  - **Filter:** the list is filtered by `Address!filter(a | a!container(CustomerAccount).email == …)`.
- **Seed script:**
  - **Keycloak users:** `admin`, `customer1` and `customer2`, with the password equal to the user name.
  - **Admin row:** the `AdminAccount` row for `admin@example.com`.
  - **Realm:** it also switches on `registrationAllowed`.
  - **Gap:** none of this is possible in the model; see issue 4.
- **Tests moved:**
  - **Admin tests** live in `application/e2e/webshop__admin/playwright/tests`: seed, S04, S09, S11 admin parts, F01–F08, and the admin side of F09–F12 and F14.
  - **Customer tests** live in `application/e2e/webshop__customer/playwright/tests`: S07, S08, S10, S11 customer parts, and the customer side of F09, F11, F13–F16.
  - **Both** use the real actor logins.

### Catalog paths used (also in TRACE.md)
- **F08/F07/F12:** `ui-authoring-guide.
